, the time localization factor becomes
N_{1}^{5-2\alpha+\varepsilon}
and we need to account for the weight at negative Sobolev regularity. We obtain as a consequence of the nonlinear Loomis-Whitney inequality (
35
)

\begin{split}&\quad N^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta}N_{1}^{5-2\alpha+\varepsilon}\sum_{1\lesssim L\ll N_{1}^{\alpha}N}L^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|1_{D_{N,L}}(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\\
&\lesssim N_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}N^{-\delta}N_{1}^{s_{1}(\alpha,\varepsilon,\mathbb{R}^{2})}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}\lesssim N_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}N_{1}^{s_{1}(\alpha,\varepsilon+\delta,\mathbb{R}^{2})}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}\end{split}

as long as
N\gtrsim N_{1}^{-2}
. For
N\lesssim N_{1}^{-2}
we can apply a bilinear Strichartz estimate from Lemma
4.3

after invoking duality to find

\begin{split}&\quad N^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta}N_{1}^{5-2\alpha+\varepsilon}\sum_{1\lesssim L\ll N_{1}^{\alpha}N}L^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|1_{D_{N,L}}(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\\
&\lesssim N^{\frac{5}{4}-\delta}N_{1}^{\frac{5-2\alpha+\varepsilon}{2}}\log(N_{1})\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}.\end{split}

This clearly suffices.

(ii)
L_{\max}\gtrsim N_{1}^{\alpha}N
: We further distinguish between
L_{\max}=L
and
L_{\max}=L_{1}(\text{ or }L_{2}
).

\bullet~L_{\max}=L
: We apply Hölder’s inequality and two
L^{4}
-Strichartz estimates from Proposition
3.1
:

\begin{split}&\quad N^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta}N_{1}^{5-2\alpha+\varepsilon}L^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|1_{D_{N,L}}(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\\
&\lesssim N^{-\delta}N_{1}^{5-2\alpha+\varepsilon}(N_{1}^{\alpha}N)^{-\frac{1}{2}}N_{1}^{\frac{2-\alpha}{4}}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim N^{-\delta}N_{1}^{\frac{11}{2}-\frac{11\alpha}{4}+\varepsilon}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

Like above this suffices for
N\gtrsim N_{1}^{-2}
. For
N\ll N_{1}^{-2}
we can argue again using a bilinear Strichartz estimate from Lemma
4.3
.

\bullet~L_{\max}=L_{1}(\text{or }L_{2})
We assume
L_{\text{med}}\ll N_{1}^{\alpha}N
. Invoking duality and Lemma
4.4
gives

\begin{split}&\quad N^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta}N_{1}^{5-2\alpha+\varepsilon}\sum_{1\lesssim L\ll N_{1}^{\alpha}N}L^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|1_{D_{N,L}}(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\\
&\lesssim N^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta}N_{1}^{5-2\alpha+\varepsilon}N_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3\alpha}{4}}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}\\
&\lesssim N_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}N^{-\delta}N_{1}^{s_{1}(\alpha,\varepsilon,\mathbb{R}^{2})}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}.\end{split}

This estimate suffices for
N_{1}^{-2}\lesssim N\lesssim 1
. For
N\lesssim N_{1}^{-2}
we can again conclude by Lemma
4.3
.

(2) Resonant case
(
1\lesssim N\ll N_{1},L_{\max}\ll N_{1}^{\alpha}N
): We find from increasing time localization and modulation localization that it suffices to show estimates

(76)

\begin{split}&\quad N^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\frac{N_{1}}{N}\big)^{5-2\alpha+\varepsilon}\sum_{L\geqslant N^{5-2\alpha+\varepsilon}}L^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|1_{D_{N,L}}(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\\
&\lesssim N_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}N^{-c(\varepsilon)}N_{1}^{s_{1}(\alpha,C\varepsilon)}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}\end{split}

for
L_{i}\geqslant N_{1}^{5-2\alpha+\varepsilon}
.
First, we suppose that
L_{\max}\ll N_{1}^{\alpha}N
. Since this case is resonant, we can apply the nonlinear Loomis-Whitney inequality (
35
) (as a special case of Lemma
7.5
) to find

\begin{split}&\quad N^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\frac{N_{1}}{N}\big)^{5-2\alpha+\varepsilon}L^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|1_{D_{N,L}}(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\\
&\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3\alpha}{4}}\big(\frac{N_{1}}{N}\big)^{5-2\alpha+\varepsilon}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}.\end{split}

This estimate implies (
76
) with
C=1
.

(3) Non-resonant case
(
L_{\max}\gtrsim N_{1}^{\alpha}N
,
1\lesssim N\ll N_{1}\sim N_{2}
): Depending on
L_{\max}
, we have the following sub-cases:

(i)
L_{\max}=L
. We find from applying Hölder’s inequality and two
L^{4}
-Strichartz estimates from Proposition
3.1
:

\begin{split}&\quad N^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\frac{N_{1}}{N}\big)^{5-2\alpha+\varepsilon}\sum_{L\geqslant N_{1}^{\alpha}N}L^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|1_{D_{N,L}}(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\\
&\lesssim N^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\frac{N_{1}}{N}\big)^{5-2\alpha+\varepsilon}(N_{1}^{\alpha}N)^{-\frac{1}{2}}N_{1}^{\frac{2-\alpha}{4}}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}\\
&=N_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}N^{-(5-2\alpha+\varepsilon)}N_{1}^{s_{1}(\alpha,\varepsilon,\mathbb{R}^{2})}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}\end{split}

This implies (
76
).

(ii)
L_{\max}=L_{1}
and
L_{\text{med}}\ll N_{1}^{\alpha}N
: In this case, we invoke duality and apply Lemma
7.5
to find

(77)

\begin{split}&\quad N^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\frac{N_{1}}{N}\big)^{5-2\alpha+\varepsilon}\sum_{N^{5-2\alpha+\varepsilon}\leqslant L\leqslant N_{1}^{\alpha}N}L^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|1_{D_{N,L}}(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\\
&\lesssim N^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\frac{N_{1}}{N}\big)^{5-2\alpha+\varepsilon}\log(N_{1})N_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3\alpha}{4}}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}.\end{split}

This implies again (
76
).

The easier case when
L_{\text{med}}\gtrsim N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2}
(
76
) follows from applying Lemma
4.3
.
This completes the proof of Proposition
7.2
.

\hfill\Box

7.4.
Energy estimates

This section is devoted to the proof of Proposition
7.4
. Energy estimates for solutions with the claimed regularity were already proved in
[
33
]
. We need to establish estimates for differences of solutions
v=u_{1}-u_{2}
:

(78)

\|v\|^{2}_{\bar{B}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(T)}\lesssim\|v(0)\|^{2}_{\bar{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|v\|^{2}_{\bar{F}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(T)}(\|u_{1}\|_{F^{s}(T)}+\|u_{2}\|_{F^{s}(T)})

for

s\geqslant s_{1}(\alpha,\varepsilon,\mathbb{R}^{2})=\begin{cases}6-\frac{11\alpha}{4}+\varepsilon,\quad&\alpha\in(2,\frac{24}{11}],\\
0,\quad&\alpha\in(\frac{24}{11},\frac{5}{2}).\end{cases}

We turn to the proof. For small frequencies we have by the definition of the function spaces

\|P_{\lesssim 1}v\|_{\bar{B}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(T)}\lesssim\|P_{\lesssim 1}v(0)\|_{\bar{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}.

For
N\gg 1
we invoke the fundamental theorem of calculus:

\|P_{N}v(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}=\|P_{N}v(0)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}P_{N}v\partial_{x}(P_{N}(v(u_{1}+u_{2}))dxds.

For
i=1,2
, we take a paraproduct decomposition:

(79)

P_{N}(vu_{i})=P_{N}(vP_{\ll N}u_{i})+P_{N}(P_{\ll N}v\cdot u_{i})+P_{N}(P_{\gtrsim N}vP_{\gtrsim N}u_{i}).

The first term can be estimated like in (
72
) by integration by parts and a commutator estimate. The details are omitted. The second term is different because we cannot integrate by parts and for the third term we need to estimate
P_{\gtrsim N}v
at negative regularity.

We turn to the estimate of the second term in (
79
). After adding time localization and taking the negative regularity into account, it remains to estimate

N_{1}^{5-2\alpha+\varepsilon}\iint_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}\gamma^{3}(N_{1}(t-t_{I}))P_{N_{1}}vP_{N_{2}}vP_{N_{3}}udxdt.

Above
u
denotes a solution
u_{i}
,
N_{1}\sim N_{3}\gg N_{2}
, and
\gamma\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
denotes a suitable bump function adapted to the unit interval.

After changing to Fourier space and modulation localization, this reduces again to establishing convolution estimates

\int(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})f_{3,N_{3},L_{3}}d\xi d\eta d\tau\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3\alpha}{4}}N_{2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{3}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}.

This is the content of Lemma
4.4
. The above display gives (
78
) for
s\geqslant s_{1}(\alpha,2\varepsilon,\mathbb{R}^{2})
.

Lastly, we estimate the third term in (
79
). After the usual time and modulation localization, we need to prove for
1\lesssim N_{1}\lesssim N_{2}
and
L_{i}\gtrsim N_{2}^{5-2\alpha+\varepsilon}
:

N_{2}^{5-2\alpha+\varepsilon}\int(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})f_{3,N_{3},L_{3}}d\xi d\eta d\tau\lesssim N_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}N_{2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}N_{2}^{s_{2}(\alpha,\varepsilon,\mathbb{R}^{2})}\prod_{i=1}^{3}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}.

Summaation of the above in the spatial frequencies leads to (
78
) for
s\geqslant s_{1}(\alpha,2\varepsilon,\mathbb{R}^{2})
. The above display follows from Lemma
7.5
.
The proof of (
78
) is complete.

It remains to prove (
74
):

\|v\|^{2}_{B^{r}(T)}\lesssim\|v(0)\|^{2}_{H^{r,0}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|v\|^{3}_{F^{r}(T)}+\|v\|_{\bar{F}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(T)}\|v\|_{F^{r}(T)}\|u_{2}\|_{F^{2r+\frac{1}{2}}(T)}

for
r\geqslant s_{1}(\alpha,\varepsilon,\mathbb{R}^{2})
.
To this end, we invoke again the fundamental theorem of calculus to find for
N\gg 1

\begin{split}&\quad\|P_{N}v(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\\
&=\|P_{N}v(0)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}P_{N}v\partial_{x}(P_{N}(v(u_{1}+u_{2})))dxds\\
&=\|P_{N}v(0)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}P_{N}v\partial_{x}(P_{N}(v^{2}))dxds+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}P_{N}v\partial_{x}(P_{N}(vu_{2}))dxds.\end{split}

The first integral can be estimated like solutions in (
72
). It suffices to estimate the second integral. We carry out a paraproduct decomposition:

P_{N}(vu_{2})=P_{N}(vP_{\ll N}u_{2})+P_{N}(P_{\ll N}v\cdot u_{2})+P_{N}(P_{\gtrsim N}v\cdot P_{\gtrsim N}u_{2}).

The first term can be estimated like in (
72
) by integration by parts. We turn to the second term, for which we need to show estimates after adding time localization and dyadic frequency localization for
N_{1}\sim N_{3}\gg N_{2}
,
N_{1}\gtrsim 1
:

N_{1}^{5-2\alpha+\varepsilon}\iint_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}\gamma^{3}(N_{1}(t-t_{I}))P_{N_{1}}vP_{N_{2}}vP_{N_{3}}udxdt.

After changing to Fourier space and modulation localization, it suffices to show

\int(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})f_{3,N_{3},L_{3}}d\xi d\eta d\tau\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3\alpha}{4}}N_{2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{3}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}.

This establishes (
74
) for
r\geqslant s_{1}(\alpha,2\varepsilon,\mathbb{R}^{2})
.
The above display is again Lemma
7.5
.

For the last term we need to estimate for

N_{1}\ll N_{2}\sim N_{3}
,
N_{1}\gtrsim 1
:

N_{2}^{5-2\alpha+\varepsilon}\iint_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}\gamma^{3}(N_{1}(t-t_{I}))P_{N_{1}}vP_{N_{2}}vP_{N_{3}}udxdt.

Like above we change to Fourier space and carry out a modulation localization such that it suffices to show

\int(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})f_{3,N_{3},L_{3}}d\xi d\eta d\tau\lesssim N_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3\alpha}{4}}N_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{3}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}

as this will imply (
74
) for
r\geqslant s_{1}(\alpha,2\varepsilon,\mathbb{R}^{2})
. The above display is once more established by Lemma
7.5
. The proof is complete.

\hfill\Box

8.
Quasilinear well-posedness on the cylinder

In this section we show the following result on quasilinear local well-posedness on the cylinder. Recall the definition of
s_{1}(\alpha,\varepsilon,\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})
:

s_{1}(\alpha,\varepsilon,\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})=\begin{cases}\frac{11}{6}-\frac{2\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon,&\quad 2<\alpha\leqslant\frac{11}{4}\text{ and }\varepsilon>0,\\
0,&\quad\frac{11}{4}<\alpha\leqslant 5.\end{cases}

Theorem 8.1
.

Let
\mathbb{D}=\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}
,
\alpha\in(2,5)
, and
s\geqslant s_{1}(\alpha,\varepsilon,\mathbb{D})
with
s_{1}
defined in (
9
) and
\varepsilon>0
. Then, there is a data-to-solution mapping
S_{T}^{s}:H^{s,0}(\mathbb{D})\to C_{T}H^{s,0}(\mathbb{D})
, which assigns real-valued initial data
u_{0}\in H^{s,0}(\mathbb{D})
to solutions
u\in C_{T}H^{s,0}
to (
1
) and
T=T(\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s,0}})
is lower semi-continuous. It holds
T(\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s,0}})\gtrsim 1
for
\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s,0}(\mathbb{D})}\downarrow 0
.

8.1.
Outline of the proof.

Like in the previous section on KP-I equations posed on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, we use short-time Fourier restriction.

We have the following short-time bilinear estimates:

Proposition 8.2
.

Let
\alpha\in(2,5)
and
T=T(N)=N^{-\frac{5-\alpha}{3}-\varepsilon(\alpha)}
with

0<\varepsilon<\frac{4\alpha}{6}-\frac{4}{3}.

Then the estimate

(80)

\|\partial_{x}(uv)\|_{\mathcal{N}^{s}(T)}\lesssim T^{\kappa}(\|u\|_{F^{s}(T)}\|v\|_{F^{0}(T)}+\|u\|_{F^{0}(T)}\|v\|_{F^{s}(T)}

holds for
s\geqslant 0
.

Secondly, the estimate

(81)

\|\partial_{x}(uv)\|_{\bar{\mathcal{N}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(T)}\lesssim T^{\kappa}\|u\|_{F^{s}(T)}\|v\|_{\bar{F}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(T)}

holds for
s\geqslant s_{1}(\alpha,\varepsilon,\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})
.

Note that we obtain the following as a special case:

\|\partial_{x}(u_{1}u_{2})\|_{\mathcal{N}^{0}(T)}\lesssim T^{\kappa}\|u_{1}\|_{F^{0}(T)}\|u_{2}\|_{F^{0}(T)}.

The proof will be given in Subsection
8.3
. The veracity of (
80
) will give an upper bound for
\varepsilon
.

A further ingredient is the short-time energy propagation. We show energy estimates for the solution:

(82)

\|u\|^{2}_{B^{s}(T)}\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s,0}}^{2}+T^{\delta}\|u\|_{F^{s}(T)}^{2}\|u\|_{F^{s_{1}}(T)}

for
s\geqslant s_{1}(\alpha,5\varepsilon,\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})
and
\delta=\delta(\varepsilon)>0
.

Secondly, we prove energy estimates for the difference of solutions. Let
u_{i}
,
i=1,2
be two solutions to (
1
) and let
v=u_{1}-u_{2}
. We observe that
v
solves:

\partial_{t}v-\partial_{x}D_{x}^{\alpha}v-\partial_{x}^{-1}\partial_{y}^{2}v=\frac{\partial_{x}(v(u_{1}+u_{2}))}{2}.

We shall estimate
v
in weighted Sobolev norms of negative regularity. Define

\|f\|^{2}_{\bar{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}}(1+|\xi|^{-1})^{1+2\varepsilon}\langle\xi\rangle^{-1}|\hat{f}(\xi,\eta)|^{2}d\xi(d\eta)_{1}.

The energy estimates for solutions read as follows:

Proposition 8.3
.

Suppose that
s\geqslant s_{1}
.
Then the estimate (
82
) holds true.

We formulate the estimates for differences of solutions:

Proposition 8.4
.

Let
\alpha\in(2,5)
, and
s\geqslant s_{1}
. Then choosing
\varepsilon
small enough, for
T=T(N)=N^{-\frac{5-\alpha}{3}-\varepsilon}
, the following estimate holds for some
\delta=\delta(\varepsilon)>0
:

(83)

\|v\|_{\bar{B}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(T)}^{2}\lesssim\|v(0)\|_{\bar{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}+T^{\delta}\|v\|_{\bar{F}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(T)}^{2}\big(\|u_{1}\|_{F^{s}(T)}+\|u_{2}\|_{F^{s}(T)}\big).

Let
r\geqslant s_{1}(\alpha,5\varepsilon,\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})
. The following estimate holds for some
\delta=\delta(\varepsilon)>0
:

(84)

\|v\|_{B^{r}(T)}^{2}\lesssim\|v(0)\|_{H^{r,0}}^{2}+T^{\delta}\|v\|_{F^{r}(T)}^{3}+T^{\delta}\|v\|_{\bar{F}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(T)}\|v\|_{F^{r}(T)}\|u_{2}\|_{F^{2r+\frac{1}{2}}(T)}.

With these estimates at hand, the proof follows along the same lines of Sections
6
and
7
. Details are omitted to avoid repetition.

8.2.
A trilinear convolution estimate

In this section we show the analog of Lemma
7.5
:

Lemma 8.5
.

Let
\alpha\geqslant 2
,
N_{i}\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}}
with
N_{2}\lesssim N_{1}\sim N_{3}
,
N_{1}\gtrsim 1
, and
L_{\text{med}}\gtrsim(1\vee N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon})
. Let
f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}:\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geqslant 0}
with
\text{supp}(f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}})\subseteq D_{N_{i},L_{i}}
for
i\in\{1,2,3\}
. Then the following estimate holds for some
0<\delta\leqslant\delta^{\prime}
:

(85)

\int(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})f_{3,N_{3},L_{3}}d\xi d\eta d\tau\lesssim N_{2}^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta}N_{1}^{-\frac{1}{3}-\frac{\alpha}{3}-\frac{\varepsilon}{4}+\delta}L_{\max}^{-\delta}\prod_{i=1}^{3}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{L^{2}}.

Proof.

Firstly, we can suppose that
N_{2}\gtrsim N_{1}^{-C}
for some large
C
since the estimate of tiny frequencies
N_{2}\ll N_{1}^{-C}
is immediate from Lemma
4.3
.

Next, suppose that
L_{\max}\ll N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2}
. In this case (
85
) is immediate from the nonlinear Loomis-Whitney inequality (
36
) and the lower bound on
L_{\text{med}}
:

\begin{split}&\quad\int(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})f_{3,N_{3},L_{3}}d\xi d\eta d\tau\\
&\lesssim N_{2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}N_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}}(L_{\min}L_{\max})^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle L_{\text{med}}/N_{1}^{\alpha/2}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{3}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

Next, suppose that
L_{\max}\gtrsim N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2}
. In case
L_{2}=L_{\max}
we carry out a dyadic decomposition in the transversality

D\sim\big|\frac{\eta_{1}}{\xi_{1}}-\frac{\eta_{3}}{\xi_{3}}\big|.

We remark that we have by convolution constraint for
(\xi_{i},\eta_{i})\in\pi_{\xi,\eta}\text{supp}(f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}})
:

\big|\frac{\eta_{1}}{\xi_{1}}-\frac{\eta_{2}}{\xi_{2}}\big|\lesssim\big(\frac{L_{\max}}{N_{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Indeed, we have

\Omega_{\alpha}=(\tau-\omega_{\alpha}(\xi,\eta))-(\tau_{1}-\omega_{\alpha}(\xi_{1},\eta_{1}))-(\tau_{2}-\omega_{\alpha}(\xi_{2},\eta_{2})).

This gives the claimed bound on the transversality:

\frac{|\eta_{1}\xi_{2}-\eta_{2}\xi_{1}|^{2}}{\xi_{1}\xi_{2}(\xi_{1}+\xi_{2})}\lesssim L_{\max}\Rightarrow\big|\frac{\eta_{1}}{\xi_{1}}-\frac{\eta_{2}}{\xi_{2}}\big|\lesssim\big(\frac{L_{\max}}{N_{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

The summation in
D
incurs a factor of
\log(L_{\max})+\log(N_{1})
by the lower bound on
N_{2}
. For
D\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}
we can apply Hölder’s inequality and two
L^{4}
-Strichartz estimates from Proposition
3.3
to find

\begin{split}&\quad\int(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})f_{3,N_{3},L_{3}}d\xi d\eta d\tau\\
&\leqslant\|f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\prod_{i=1,3}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{t,x,y}[f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}]\|_{L^{4}_{t,x,y}}\\
&\lesssim_{\varepsilon}(N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta}L_{\max}^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta}N_{1}^{\frac{2-\alpha}{4}+\varepsilon}\|f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\prod_{i=1,3}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

Here we use an almost orthogonal decomposition to

\big|\frac{\eta_{i}}{\xi_{i}}-A\big|\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}

such that Proposition
3.3
becomes applicable.

For
D\gtrsim N_{1}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}
we can apply a bilinear Strichartz estimate from Proposition
4.1
to find

\begin{split}&\quad\int(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})f_{3,N_{3},L_{3}}d\xi d\eta d\tau\\
&\leqslant\|f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*\tilde{f}_{3,N_{3},L_{3}}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\\
&\lesssim(N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}L_{\max}^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{\min}^{\frac{1}{2}}N_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle L_{\text{med}}/N_{1}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{3}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}\\
&\lesssim N_{1}^{-\frac{5}{6}-\frac{\alpha}{3}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{3}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}.\end{split}

Next, we consider
L_{1}=L_{\max}\gtrsim N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2}
(which covers as well
L_{3}=L_{\max}
by symmetry). We apply Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
4.5
to find

\begin{split}&\quad\int(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})f_{3,N_{3},L_{3}}d\xi d\eta d\tau\\
&\leqslant\|f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{2}\|f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}}*\tilde{f}_{3,N_{3},L_{3}}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\\
&\lesssim(N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta}L_{\max}^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta}\log(L_{\max})N_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{\min}^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle L_{\text{med}}/N_{1}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{3}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}.\end{split}

The claim follows by the lower bound on
L_{\text{med}}
.

∎

We remark that by interpolation with Lemma
4.3
we can always obtain a small power of
N_{2}
and smoothing in
L_{\max}
:

\int(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})f_{3,N_{3},L_{3}}d\xi d\eta d\tau\lesssim N_{2}^{-\frac{1}{2}+}N_{1}^{-\frac{1}{3}-\frac{\alpha}{3}-\frac{\varepsilon}{4}}L_{\max}^{0-}\prod_{i=1}^{3}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}.

8.3.
Short-time bilinear estimates

In this section we shall obtain short-time nonlinear estimates

(86)

\|\partial_{x}(u_{1}u_{2})\|_{\mathcal{N}^{s}}\lesssim T^{\kappa}(\|u_{1}\|_{F^{s}}\|u_{2}\|_{F^{0}}+\|u_{1}\|_{F^{0}}\|u_{2}\|_{F^{s}})

and modified estimates in weighted norms for differences of solutions. The small power of
T
is a consequence of Lemma
2.3
from a smoothing in the maximal modulation
L_{\max}^{0-}
, see the remark above. We turn to the proof of Proposition
8.2
.

8.3.1.
High
\times
Low
\to
High interaction

In this case we suppose that
N\sim N_{1}\gtrsim N_{2}
.
We focus in the following on
N\gtrsim 1
.
The case
N\lesssim 1
can be estimated via Lemma
4.3
.
The following reductions to convolution estimates in Fourier variables are carried out like in previous sections.
We shall establish an estimate

(87)

\|P_{N}\partial_{x}(P_{N_{1}}u_{1}P_{N_{2}}u_{2})\|_{\mathcal{N}_{N}(T)}\lesssim C(N,N_{1},N_{2})\|P_{N_{1}}u_{1}\|_{F_{N_{1}}(T)}\|P_{N_{2}}u_{2}\|_{F_{N_{2}}(T)}.

The claim then follows from dyadic summations.

In the following we consider extensions
P_{N_{i}}\tilde{u}_{i}
of
P_{N_{i}}u_{i}
such that

\|P_{N_{i}}\tilde{u}_{i}\|_{F_{N_{i}}}\leqslant 2\|P_{N_{i}}u_{i}\|_{F_{N_{i}}(T)}.

To lighten notations, we shall again redenote
\tilde{u}_{i}\to u_{i}
.

We use the definition of the
\mathcal{N}_{N}
-norm to bound
the left hand side of (
87
) by

\begin{split}&\sup_{t_{N}\in\mathbb{R}}\big\|(\tau-\omega_{\alpha}(\xi,\eta)+iN^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon})^{-1}\xi 1_{A_{N}}(\xi)\mathcal{F}[u_{1,N_{1}}\cdot\eta_{0}(N^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}(t-t_{N}))]\\
&\quad*\mathcal{F}[u_{2,N_{2}}\cdot\eta_{0}(N^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}(t-t_{N}))]\big\|_{X_{N}}.\end{split}

We define

f_{i,N_{i}}=\mathcal{F}[u_{i,N_{i}}\cdot\eta_{0}(N^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}(t-t_{N}))].

Consider
L_{1},L_{2}\geqslant N^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}
and
f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}:\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
are functions supported in
D_{N_{i},L_{i}}
and for
L_{i}=N^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}
in
D_{N,\leqslant L_{i}}
. By the function space properties it suffices to
obtain the following estimate for
L\geqslant N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}
:

N_{1}\|1_{D_{\alpha,N,L}}(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\lesssim C(N_{1},N_{2})L_{\max}^{0-}L^{\frac{1}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}.

We shall see that for the High-Low interaction, any
\varepsilon>0
suffices to obtain the estimate in the above display with
C(N_{1},N_{2})=N_{1}^{-\varepsilon^{\prime}}
. Then
it follows from dyadic summation that (
80
) and (
81
) are true. As seen in the previous section, suitable interpolation with Lemma
4.3
allows us always to squeeze out a factor
N_{\min}^{0+}L_{\max}^{0-}
, for which reason we obtain summability in the low frequency and modulation parameters. This will often be omitted not to overburden the notation.
We remark that it is the High
\times
High
\to
Low interaction (
N\ll N_{1}\sim N_{2}
) which limits the regularity
s\geqslant s_{1}
in (
81
).

Note that by the time localization we have
L_{i}\gtrsim N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}
. Below we shall distinguish the cases of

(1)

very low frequencies:
N_{2}\lesssim 1\lesssim N_{1}
,
N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2}\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}}
,

(2)

resonant case:
L_{\max}\ll N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2}
,
N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2}\gtrsim N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}}
,

(3)

non-resonant case:
L_{\max}\gtrsim N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2}
,
N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2}\gtrsim N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}}
.

(1) Very low frequencies
(
N_{2}\lesssim 1\lesssim N_{1},N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2}\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}}
): We estimate

N_{1}\|1_{D_{N,L}}(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}

by bilinear Strichartz estimates recorded in Lemma
4.3
. This gives the (crude) estimate

\|1_{D_{N,L}}(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\lesssim N_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{\max}^{0-}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}.

Taking into account the derivative loss
N_{1}
and
L\geqslant N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}
we obtain

N_{1}\|1_{D_{N,L}}(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\lesssim N_{1}^{1-\frac{5-\alpha}{6}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}N_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{\max}^{0-}L^{\frac{1}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}.

Summing over
N_{2}\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}-\alpha}
and
L
with weight
L^{-\frac{1}{2}}
this estimate is sufficient.

In the following cases, we shall consider exclusively
N_{2}N_{1}^{\alpha}\gtrsim N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}}
, which imposes a lower bound on
N_{2}
.

(2) Resonant case
(
L_{\max}=\max(L,L_{1},L_{2})\ll N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2},\quad 1\lesssim N\sim N_{1},\quad N_{2}\gtrsim N_{1}^{-\alpha+\frac{5-\alpha}{3}}
): We consider two sub-cases:

(i)
N_{1}^{\frac{4-2\alpha}{3}}\lesssim N_{2}
: Applying the nonlinear Loomis-Whitney inequality (
36
) gives

\begin{split}&\quad N_{1}\sum_{N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}\leqslant L\leqslant N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2}}L^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|1_{D_{N,L}}(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{N_{1}^{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}{N_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\sum_{N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}\leqslant L\leqslant N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2}}L^{-\frac{1}{2}}L^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}N_{1}^{-\frac{5-\alpha}{6}-\varepsilon}\|f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\|f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{N_{1}^{\frac{2-\alpha}{3}}}{N_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\log(N_{1})N_{1}^{-\varepsilon}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

This yields a favorable estimate provided that
N_{2}\gtrsim N_{1}^{-\kappa}
for
\kappa=\frac{2(\alpha-2)}{3}
.

(ii)
N_{2}\ll N_{1}^{\frac{4-2\alpha}{3}}
: We find from the short-time bilinear Strichartz estimate recorded in Proposition
4.1

\begin{split}&\quad N_{1}\sum_{N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}\leqslant L\leqslant N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2}}L^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|1_{D_{N,L}}(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\\
&\lesssim N_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}N_{1}^{1-\frac{5-\alpha}{3}-\varepsilon}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\lesssim N_{1}^{-\varepsilon}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}.\end{split}

(3) Non-resonant case
(
L_{\max}\geqslant N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2}
,
N_{2}\gtrsim N_{1}^{-\alpha+\frac{5-\alpha}{3}}
): We consider further sub-cases:

(i)
N_{2}\gtrsim 1
: Depending on
L_{\max}
, we have the following cases:

\bullet~L_{\max}=L
: The case
L_{1}=L_{\max}
(the high frequency is at high modulation) can be handled by dual arguments. Indeed, there is additional smoothing
N_{1}^{-\varepsilon}
in the estimates below, which allows us to handle the summation over
L
.

We remark that we have by convolution constraint for
(\xi_{i},\eta_{i})\in\pi_{\xi,\eta}\text{supp}(f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}})
:

\big|\frac{\eta_{1}}{\xi_{1}}-\frac{\eta_{2}}{\xi_{2}}\big|\lesssim\big(\frac{L_{\max}}{N_{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Indeed, we have

\Omega_{\alpha}=(\tau-\omega_{\alpha}(\xi,\eta))-(\tau_{1}-\omega_{\alpha}(\xi_{1},\eta_{1}))-(\tau_{2}-\omega_{\alpha}(\xi_{2},\eta_{2})).

This gives the claimed bound on the transversality:

\frac{|\eta_{1}\xi_{2}-\eta_{2}\xi_{1}|^{2}}{\xi_{1}\xi_{2}(\xi_{1}+\xi_{2})}\lesssim L_{\max}\Rightarrow\big|\frac{\eta_{1}}{\xi_{1}}-\frac{\eta_{2}}{\xi_{2}}\big|\lesssim\big(\frac{L_{\max}}{N_{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

For this reason we can apply a bilinear Strichartz estimate from Lemma
4.5
and take into account the minimum modulation size to find

\begin{split}&\quad N_{1}L^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|1_{D_{N,L}}(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\\
&\lesssim N_{1}(N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta}N_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}N_{1}^{-\frac{5-\alpha}{6}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}\\
&\lesssim N_{2}^{\delta}N_{1}^{\frac{1}{6}-\frac{\alpha}{3}+\delta-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}.\end{split}

This is acceptable choosing
\delta=\delta(\varepsilon)>0
small enough.

\bullet~L_{\max}=L_{2}
: If
L\gtrsim N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2}
we can apply a bilinear Strichartz estimate from Lemma
4.3
to find

\begin{split}&\quad\sum_{L\gtrsim N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2}}L^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|1_{D_{N,L}}(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\\
&\lesssim(N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}N_{2}^{\frac{3}{4}}(N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2})^{-\frac{1}{4}+\delta}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}\\
&\lesssim N_{1}^{-\frac{3\alpha}{4}+\delta\alpha}N_{2}^{\delta}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}.\end{split}

Hence, we suppose
L\ll N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2}
in the following.

An application of Lemma
8.5
after invoking duality gives

\|1_{D_{N,L}}(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\lesssim L_{\max}^{-\delta}N_{2}^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta}N_{1}^{-\frac{1}{3}-\frac{\alpha}{3}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}.

This is acceptable for
N_{2}\gtrsim 1
.

(ii)
N_{2}\lesssim 1
:

\bullet~L_{\max}=L~(\text{or }L_{1})
: In this case, the previous estimates suffice.

\bullet~L_{\max}=L_{2}\gtrsim N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2}\gtrsim N_{1}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}
. We impose an upper bound
L\lesssim N_{1}^{3}
. In this case we estimate by duality and Lemma
4.3
:

(88)

\begin{split}&\quad\|1_{D_{N,L}}(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\\
&=\sup_{\|g_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}=1}}\int g_{N,L}(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})\\
&=\sup_{\|g_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}=1}}\int(\tilde{g}_{N,L}*f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}})f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}}\\
&\lesssim\sup_{\|g_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}=1}}\|\tilde{g}_{N,L}*f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\|f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\\
&\lesssim N_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}N_{1}^{\frac{1}{4}}(LL_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}N_{1}^{-\frac{(5-\alpha)}{12}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}L_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}(N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{2}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}.\end{split}

Summing (
88
) over
N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2}\lesssim L\lesssim N_{1}^{3}
and
N_{1}^{-C}\lesssim N_{2}\lesssim 1
gives

\begin{split}&\quad N_{1}\sum_{N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2}\lesssim L\lesssim N_{1}^{3}}L^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|1_{D_{N,L}}(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\\
&\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{10}{12}-\frac{5\alpha}{12}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}\log(N_{1})\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}.\end{split}

This is acceptable.

In case
L\gtrsim N_{1}^{3}
we can apply Lemma
4.3
on
f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}}
:

\begin{split}&\quad N_{1}\sum_{L\gtrsim N_{1}^{3}}L^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|1_{D_{N,L}}(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\\
&\lesssim N_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}N_{2}^{\frac{3}{4}}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}.\end{split}

This is sufficient.

Interpolation with the bilinear Strichartz estimate from Lemma
4.3
yields factors
N_{2}^{0+}L_{\max}^{0-}
, which finishes the argument for the High
\times
Low
\to
High interaction.

8.3.2.
High
\times
High
\to
Low interaction

Here, reductions to frequency and modulation localized estimates require additional time localization. We shall estimate for
N_{1}\sim N_{2}\gg N
:

\begin{split}&\|P_{N}\partial_{x}(P_{N_{1}}u_{1}P_{N_{2}}u_{2})\|_{\mathcal{N}_{N}}=\sup_{t_{N}\in\mathbb{R}}\|(\tau-\omega_{\alpha}(\xi,\eta)+iN^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}_{+})^{-1}\\
&\quad\times\xi\mathcal{F}_{t,x,y}[\eta_{0}(N_{+}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}(t-t_{N}))P_{N_{1}}u_{1}P_{N_{2}}u_{2}]\|_{X_{N}}.\end{split}

Since
N_{1}\sim N_{2}\gg N
, the time localization
N_{+}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}
does not suffice for
N_{1}\gg 1
. We need to additionally localize to time intervals to size
N_{1,+}^{-(\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon)}
. To this end, let
\gamma\in C^{\infty}_{c}(-2,2)
with

\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\gamma^{2}(t-k)\equiv 1.

We write

\begin{split}&\quad\eta_{0}(N_{+}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}(t-t_{N})P_{N_{1}}u_{1}P_{N_{2}}u_{2})\\
&=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\eta_{0}(N_{+}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}(t-t_{N}))(\gamma(N_{1,+}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}t-k)P_{N_{1}}u_{1})(\gamma(N_{1,+}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}t-k)P_{N_{2}}u_{2})).\end{split}

Note that

\#\{k\in\mathbb{Z}:\eta_{0}(N_{+}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}(t-t_{N}))\gamma(N_{1,+}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}t-k)\not\equiv 0\}\sim\Big(\frac{N_{1,+}}{N_{+}}\Big)^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}.

By Minkowski’s inequality it will suffice to estimate
\Big(\frac{N_{1,+}}{N_{+}}\Big)^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}
expressions of the kind (with estimate uniformly in
k
):

\begin{split}&\hskip 9.24994pt\|(\tau-\omega_{\alpha}(\xi,\eta)+iN_{+}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon})^{-1}i\xi 1_{A_{N}}(\xi)\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\times\mathcal{F}_{t,x,y}[\eta_{0}(N_{+}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}(t-t_{N}))(\gamma(N_{1,+}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}(t-k)P_{N_{1}}u_{1})(\gamma(N_{1,+}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}t-k)P_{N_{2}}u_{2})]\|_{X_{N}}.\end{split}

The time localization suffices to conclude

\|\mathcal{F}_{t,x}[\gamma(N_{1,+}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}(t-k))P_{N_{i}}u_{i}]\|_{X_{N_{i}}}\lesssim\|P_{N_{i}}u_{i}\|_{F_{N_{i}}}.

Let

\begin{split}f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}&=\mathcal{F}_{t,x}[\eta_{0}(N_{+}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}\gamma(N_{1,+}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}(t-k))P_{N_{1}}u_{1}],\\
f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}}&=\mathcal{F}_{t,x}[\gamma(N_{1,+}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}(t-k))P_{N_{2}}u_{2}].\end{split}

By the function space properties, we have

N\sum_{L_{i}\geqslant N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\lesssim\|\mathcal{F}_{t,x}[\gamma(N_{1,+}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}t-k)P_{N_{i}}u_{i}]\|_{X_{N_{i}}}.

Consequently, it suffices to prove dyadic estimates

\begin{split}&\quad N\Big(\frac{N_{1,+}}{N_{+}}\Big)^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}\sum_{L\geqslant N^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}}L^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|1_{D_{N,L}}(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\\
&\lesssim C(N,N_{1})\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\end{split}

for
L_{i}\gtrsim N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}
.

(1) Very small frequencies
(
N\lesssim 1\lesssim N_{1}\sim N_{2}
): We distinguish the resonant and the non-resonant cases:

(i)
L_{\max}\ll NN_{1}^{\alpha}
: We carry out a dyadic decomposition in the output frequency
N
and add time localization
N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}
. Using the nonlinear Loomis-Whitney inequality (
36
), we obtain

\begin{split}&\quad NL^{-\frac{1}{2}}N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}\|1_{D_{N,L}}(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\\
&\lesssim NN_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{6}}N_{1}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\big(\frac{N_{1}}{N}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim N^{\frac{1}{2}}N_{1}^{\frac{4}{3}-\frac{2\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

This is acceptable provided that an upper bound on
\varepsilon
,

(89)

0<\varepsilon<\frac{4\alpha}{6}-\frac{4}{3},

holds true.

(ii)
L_{\max}\gtrsim NN_{1}^{\alpha}
: Depending on
L_{\max}
, the following sub-cases arise:

\bullet~L_{\max}=L_{1}
(or
L_{2}
): Employing duality and Lemma
4.3
like in (
88
) gives

\begin{split}&\quad NL^{-\frac{1}{2}}N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}\|1_{D_{N,L}}(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\\
&\lesssim NN_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}(N_{1}^{\alpha}N)^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta}N^{\frac{3}{4}}N_{1}^{-\frac{5-\alpha}{12}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}L_{\max}^{-\delta}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim N^{\frac{1}{4}+\delta}N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{4}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\alpha\delta}L_{\max}^{-\delta}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}},\end{split}

which is likewise acceptable.

\bullet~L_{\max}=L
: In this case we apply Lemma
4.3
to
f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}}
and find

\begin{split}&\quad NN_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}L^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|1_{D_{N,L}}(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\\
&\lesssim NN_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}(N_{1}^{\alpha}N)^{-\frac{1}{2}}N^{\frac{1}{2}}N_{1}^{\frac{1}{4}}N_{1}^{-\frac{5-\alpha}{12}-\frac{\varepsilon}{4}}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\\
&\lesssim NN_{1}^{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{3\alpha}{4}+\frac{3\varepsilon}{4}}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}.\end{split}

This is acceptable provided that

(90)

0<\varepsilon<\alpha-2

holds true. Note that this is less restrictive than (
89
).

In the following cases, we assume that
N\gtrsim 1
.

(2) Resonant case
(
L_{\max}\ll N_{1}^{\alpha}N
and
1\lesssim N\ll N_{1}\sim N_{2}
): We obtain from applying the nonlinear Loomis–Whitney inequality (
36
) and taking into account the additional time localization:

(91)

\begin{split}&\quad NL^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\frac{N_{1}}{N}\Big)^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}\|1_{D_{N,L}}(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\\
&\lesssim N\Big(\frac{N_{1}}{N}\Big)^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}N_{1}^{-\frac{5-\alpha}{6}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}N_{1}^{-\alpha/2}\big(\frac{N_{1}}{N}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\\
&\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{4}{3}-\frac{4\alpha}{6}+\varepsilon}N^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{5-\alpha}{3}-\varepsilon}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}.\end{split}

Estimate (
80
) follows from dyadic summation. It is (
91
) which suggests to carry out (
81
) in
\bar{\mathcal{N}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
because

\frac{4}{3}-\frac{2\alpha}{3}+\frac{1}{2}=s_{1}(\alpha,0).

(3) Non-resonant case
(
L_{\max}\gtrsim N_{1}^{\alpha}N
):
Since
N\gtrsim 1
, applying Lemma
8.5
gives

\begin{split}&\quad NL^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\frac{N_{1}}{N}\Big)^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}\|1_{D_{N,L}}(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}\\
&\lesssim N^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{5-\alpha}{3}-\varepsilon+\delta}N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}-\frac{1}{3}-\frac{\alpha}{3}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}L_{\max}^{-\delta}\prod_{i=1}^{2}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{L^{2}},\end{split}

which is acceptable.

This completes the proof of Proposition
8.2
.
\hfill\Box

8.4.
Energy estimates

We carry out energy estimates in short-time Fourier restriction norms.

8.4.1.
Energy estimates for solutions

In this subsection we show energy estimates for the solution as stated in (
82
).

Proof of Proposition
8.3
.

Let
u\in C_{T}E^{\infty}
. By the fundamental theorem of calculus we have for any
N\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}}
:

\|P_{N}u(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\|P_{N}u(0)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}}P_{N}u\,\partial_{x}P_{N}(u^{2})dxdyds.

Note that by the definition of the function spaces
B^{s}
, the low frequencies satisfy

\|P_{\ll 1}u\|_{B^{s}(T)}^{2}=\|P_{\ll 1}u(0)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

We carry out the same reductions to take advantage of the derivative nonlinearity like in the previous sections.
It suffices to estimate

N_{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}}P_{N_{1}}uP_{N_{2}}uP_{N_{3}}u\,dxdyds\quad(N_{2}\lesssim N_{1}\sim N_{3},\;N_{1}\gtrsim 1).

We need to add time localization to estimate the expression in the correct function spaces. This amounts to a factor of
N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon(\alpha)}
. After applying Plancherel’s theorem we reduce to dyadic estimates:

N_{2}N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}\big|\int\big(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}}\big)f_{3,N_{3},L_{3}}d\xi d\eta d\tau\big|

with
L_{i}\gtrsim N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon(\alpha)}
, which is attributed to the corresponding time localization.

\bullet
High
\times
Low
\to
High interaction: In this case, we have
N_{1}\sim N\gtrsim N_{2}
,
N_{1}\gtrsim 1
. Applying Lemma
8.5

we find

\begin{split}&\quad N_{2}N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}\big|\int\big(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}}\big)f_{3,N_{3},L_{3}}d\xi d\eta d\tau\big|\\
&\lesssim N_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta}N_{1}^{\frac{4}{3}-\frac{2\alpha}{3}+\frac{3\varepsilon}{4}+\delta}L_{\max}^{-\delta}\prod_{i=1}^{3}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}.\end{split}

This is acceptable for
s\geqslant s_{1}(\alpha,\varepsilon,\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})
choosing
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(\alpha)
small enough.

\bullet
High
\times
High
\to
Low interaction: In this case, we have
1\lesssim N\sim N_{2}\ll N_{1}\sim N_{3}
. We check that the estimate in the above display suffices again. The proof is complete.

∎

8.4.2.
Energy estimates for differences of solutions

We estimate the solutions to the difference equation. At a crucial step in the analysis, we cannot integrate by parts which motivates us to estimate the difference of solutions at negative Sobolev regularity.

Proof of Proposition
8.4
.

Like above, we use the fundamental theorem of calculus to find

\|P_{N}v(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\|P_{N}v(0)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}}P_{N}v\partial_{x}P_{N}(v(u_{1}+u_{2}))dxdt.

Again, by definition of function spaces, it suffices to obtain estimates for
N\gtrsim 1
.

First, we shall show the estimate for
i=1,2
with
s\geqslant s_{1}(\alpha,\varepsilon,\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})
:

(92)

\sum_{N\gtrsim 1}N^{-1}\big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}}P_{N}v\partial_{x}P_{N}(v\,u_{i})dxdt\big|\lesssim T^{\kappa}\|v\|_{F^{-\frac{1}{2}}(T)}^{2}\|u_{i}\|_{F^{s}(T)}.

To ease notation, let
u:=u_{i}
.

After dyadic frequency localization and using commutator arguments like in the proof of Proposition
8.3
, we see that we need to estimate three expressions. The first one is given by:

N_{2}N_{1}^{-1}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}}P_{N_{1}}vP_{N_{2}}uP_{N_{3}}vdxdt,\quad N_{1}\sim N_{3}\gtrsim N_{2},

where
u
denotes a solution to (
1
). Here we have integrated by parts to shift the derivative to the low frequency.

This expression can be handled like in the energy estimates for solutions and we obtain estimates

\sum_{N_{2}\lesssim N_{1}\sim N_{3}}N_{2}N_{1}^{-1}\big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}}P_{N_{1}}vP_{N_{2}}uP_{N_{3}}vdxdt\big|\lesssim T^{\kappa}\|v\|_{\bar{F}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(T)}^{2}\|u\|_{F^{s}(T)}

for
s\geqslant s_{1}(\alpha,\varepsilon,\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})
.

The second expression is given by the High
\times
High
\to
Low interaction: For
N_{1}\sim N_{3}\gg N_{2}\gtrsim 1
, we require to estimate

N_{2}^{-1}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}}P_{N_{2}}v\partial_{x}(P_{N_{1}}uP_{N_{3}}v)dxdydt.

Again the derivative acts on the low frequency, but this time the high frequency terms include a solution (
P_{N_{1}}u
) and a difference of solutions (
P_{N_{3}}v
).

Taking the derivative into account and adding time localization we need to establish estimates for

N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}\int(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})f_{3,N_{3},L_{3}}d\xi d\eta d\tau.

We apply the estimate from Lemma
8.5
to find:

\begin{split}&\quad N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}\int(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})f_{3,N_{3},L_{3}}d\xi d\eta d\tau\\
&\lesssim N_{2}^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta}N_{1}^{\frac{4-2\alpha}{3}+\frac{3\varepsilon}{4}+\delta}L_{\max}^{-\delta}\prod_{i=1}^{3}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}.\end{split}

This estimate is summable for
s\geqslant s_{1}(\alpha,\varepsilon,\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})
.

The third expression is given by

N_{1}N_{1}^{-1}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}}P_{N_{1}}vP_{N_{2}}vP_{N_{3}}udxdt,\quad N_{1}\sim N_{3}\gtrsim N_{2}.

In this case, we cannot use integration by parts to place the derivative on the low frequency. We observe that the estimate at negative regularity comes to the rescue, compensating for the derivative loss. Moreover, the case of low frequencies
N_{1}\lesssim 1
is easy to handle because it is not necessary to add time localization and the derivative exhibits a smoothing effect.

After frequency-dependent time localization and applying Plancherel’s theorem, with the above notations it suffices to obtain summable estimates for

N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}\int(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})f_{3,N_{3},L_{3}}d\xi d\eta d\tau.

An application of Lemma
8.5
gives

\begin{split}&\quad N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}\int(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})f_{3,N_{3},L_{3}}d\xi d\eta d\tau\\
&\lesssim N_{2}^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta}N_{1}^{\frac{4}{3}-\frac{2\alpha}{3}+\frac{3\varepsilon}{4}+\delta}L_{\max}^{-\delta}\prod_{i=1}^{3}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}.\end{split}

Summation yields again (
92
).

We turn to the proof of (
84
):

\|v\|^{2}_{E^{s}(T)}\lesssim\|v(0)\|^{2}_{H^{s,0}}+T^{\kappa}\|v\|^{3}_{F^{s,0}(T)}+T^{\kappa}\|v\|_{\bar{F}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(T)}\|v\|_{F^{s}(T)}\|u_{2}\|_{F^{2s+\frac{1}{2}}(T)}.

We invoke again the fundamental theorem of calculus to find for
N\gg 1

\begin{split}&\quad\|P_{N}v(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\\
&=\|P_{N}v(0)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}P_{N}v\partial_{x}(P_{N}(v(u_{1}+u_{2})))dxds\\
&=\|P_{N}v(0)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}P_{N}v\partial_{x}(P_{N}(v^{2}))dxds+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}P_{N}v\partial_{x}(P_{N}(vu_{2}))dxds.\end{split}

For symmetry reasons the first integral can be handled like in the estimate for solutions (
82
).

For the second term we take once more a paraproduct decomposition:

(93)

P_{N}(vu_{2})=P_{N}(vP_{\ll N}u_{2})+P_{N}(P_{\ll N}v\cdot u_{2})+P_{N}(P_{\gtrsim N}vP_{\gtrsim N}u_{2}).

The first term can be handled by integration by parts and the arguments from the proof of (
82
) gives

\sum_{N\geqslant 1}N^{2s}\big|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}P_{N}v\partial_{x}(P_{N}(vP_{\ll N}u_{2}))dxdyds\big|\lesssim T^{\kappa}\|v\|^{2}_{F^{s}(T)}\|u_{2}\|_{F^{s}(T)}.

Moreover, the third term can be estimated like in (
82
) because
the derivative acts on the lowest frequency. This yields again

\sum_{N\gtrsim 1}N^{2s}\big|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}}\partial_{x}P_{N}vP_{N}(P_{\gtrsim N}vP_{\gtrsim N}u_{2}))dxdydt\big|\lesssim T^{\kappa}\|v\|_{F^{s}(T)}^{2}\|u_{2}\|_{F^{s}(T)}.

It remains to estimate the second term in (
93
). Here we need to consider

N\big|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}}P_{N_{1}}vP_{N_{2}}vP_{N_{3}}udxdyds\big|,\quad N_{2}\ll N_{1}\sim N_{3}\sim N

in a summable manner. Time localization amounts to a factor
N^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}
and after applying Plancherel’s theorem and modulation localization we need to estimate

N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+1+\varepsilon}\int(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})f_{3,N_{3},L_{3}}d\xi d\eta d\tau

for
L_{i}\gtrsim N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+\varepsilon}
. Applying Lemma
8.5
gives

\begin{split}&\quad N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+1+\varepsilon}\int(f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*f_{2,N_{2},L_{2}})f_{3,N_{3},L_{3}}d\xi d\eta d\tau\\
&\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{5-\alpha}{3}+1+\varepsilon}N_{2}^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta}N_{1}^{-\frac{1}{3}-\frac{\alpha}{3}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}+\delta}L_{\max}^{-\delta}\prod_{i=1}^{3}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}\\
&\lesssim N_{2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}N_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}+s_{1}(\alpha,\frac{3\varepsilon}{4}+2\delta,\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})}L_{\max}^{-\delta}\prod_{i=1}^{3}L_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{i,N_{i},L_{i}}\|_{2}.\end{split}

In conclusion, choosing
\varepsilon(\alpha)
small enough, we can cover any regularity
s\geqslant s_{1}
in (
83
). The proof is complete.

∎

9.
Semilinear well-posedness

In this section, we show the sharp semilinear local well-posedness for KP-I equations posed on the Euclidean plane and on the cylinder. The results are proved by invoking the contraction mapping principle in Fourier restriction spaces.

9.1.
\mathbb{R}^{2}
case

We state the following theorem which is an improvement of
[
33
, Theorem 6.1]
and proves well-posedness in the full subcritical range.

Let
s,b\in\mathbb{R}
, recall
\omega_{\alpha}(\xi,\eta)=\xi|\xi|^{\alpha}+\frac{\eta^{2}}{\xi}
, and we define the space
X^{s,b}
as closure of Schwartz functions with respect to the norm:

\|u\|_{X^{s,b}}=\|\langle\xi\rangle^{s}\langle\tau-\omega_{\alpha}(\xi,\eta)\rangle^{b}\mathcal{F}_{t,x,y}(u)\|_{L^{2}_{\tau,\xi,\eta}}.

We define for measurable
u:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{C}
:

\|u\|_{X^{s,b}_{T}}=\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tilde{u}\in X^{s,b},\\
u=\tilde{u}|_{[0,T]}\end{subarray}}\|\tilde{u}\|_{X^{s,b}}.

Theorem 9.1
.

Let
\alpha\geqslant\frac{5}{2}
and
s>1-\frac{3\alpha}{4}
. Then, there is some
b>\frac{1}{2}
such that for
T=T(\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s,0}})
, (
68
) is analytically locally well-posed in
H^{s,0}
with the solution lying in
X^{s,b}_{T}\hookrightarrow C([0,T];H^{s,0})
.

We shall be brief here and refer the reader to
[
33
, Section 6]
for the properties of the auxiliary spaces
X^{s,b}_{T}
. The proof of the theorem is concluded in Subsection
9.3
. The following estimate is crucial:

Proposition 9.2
.

Let
\alpha\geqslant\frac{5}{2}
and
s>1-\frac{3\alpha}{4}
. Then there is some
b>\frac{1}{2}
such that the following estimate is true:

(94)

\|\partial_{x}(uv)\|_{X^{s,b-1}}\lesssim\|u\|_{X^{s,b}}\|v\|_{X^{s,b}}.

Proof.

By duality and Plancherel’s theorem, we can reduce the above to proving

(95)

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\xi~\widehat{uv}\cdot\overline{\widehat{w}}d\tau d\xi d\eta\lesssim\|u\|_{X^{s,b}}\|v\|_{X^{s,b}}\|w\|_{X^{-s,1-b}}.

After a dyadic decomposition, for
N_{i}\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}},L_{i}\in 2^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
, we prove the following estimate

(96)

\begin{split}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\ast g_{N_{2},L_{2}})h_{N,L}d\tau d\xi d\eta\Big|&\lesssim C(N_{1},N_{2},N)L_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}L^{\frac{1}{2}-}\\
&\quad\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\end{split}

for a suitable summability constant
C(N_{1},N_{2},N)
which also incorporates the derivative loss from the nonlinearity. To avoid writing the integral on the left-hand side in the above display repetitively, we denote:

I:=\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\ast g_{N_{2},L_{2}})h_{N,L}d\tau d\xi d\eta\Big|.

In the following we do a case-by-case analysis depending on the size of the
x
-frequencies.

(i) High
\times
Low
\rightarrow
High
(
N_{2}\lesssim N_{1}\sim N
): We consider two more cases:

•

L_{\max}\ll N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2}
: In case
N_{1}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{1}{2}}L_{3}^{\varepsilon}\lesssim N_{2}\lesssim 1
, using the nonlinear Loomis–Whitney estimate (
35
), we obtain

\begin{split}\sum_{N_{1}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{1}{2}}L_{3}^{\varepsilon}\lesssim N_{2}\lesssim 1}I&\lesssim\sum_{N_{1}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{1}{2}}L_{3}^{\varepsilon}\lesssim N_{2}\lesssim 1}N_{1}^{-\frac{3\alpha}{4}+\frac{1}{2}}N_{2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(L_{1}L_{2}L)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\quad\quad\times\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim N_{1}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{1}{4}}(L_{1}L_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{\lesssim 1,L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

In the other case where
N_{2}\lesssim N_{1}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{1}{2}}L^{\varepsilon}
, using the bilinear Strichartz estimate (
25
), we have

\begin{split}\sum_{N_{2}\lesssim N_{1}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{1}{2}}L^{\varepsilon}}I&\lesssim\sum_{N_{2}\lesssim N_{1}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{1}{2}}L_{3}^{\varepsilon}}N_{1}^{-\frac{\alpha}{4}}N_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}(L_{1}L_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim N_{1}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{1}{4}}(L_{1}L_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}L^{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

In both cases, after considering the derivative loss and summing up, the estimate leads to (
96
). In the case
N_{2}\gtrsim 1
, we use the nonlinear Loomis–Whitney estimate (
35
) to obtain

\begin{split}\sum_{1\lesssim N_{2}\lesssim N_{1}}I&\lesssim\sum_{1\lesssim N_{2}\lesssim N_{1}}N_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3\alpha}{4}}N_{2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(L_{1}L_{2}L)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim N_{1}^{1-\frac{3\alpha}{4}-s+(\alpha+1)\varepsilon}\sum_{1\lesssim N_{2}\lesssim N_{1}}\Big(\frac{N_{2}}{N_{1}}\Big)^{-\frac{1}{2}-s+\varepsilon}(L_{1}L_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}L^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}N_{2}^{s}N^{-1}\\
&\quad\quad\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

•

L_{\max}\gtrsim N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2}
: Using
[
33
, Lemma 4.6]
, we have

\begin{split}I&\lesssim\frac{(L_{1}L_{2}L)^{\frac{1}{2}}}{L_{\max}^{\frac{1}{4}}}N_{1}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}N_{2}^{\frac{1}{4}}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim(L_{1}L_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{\max}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}N_{1}^{-\frac{3\alpha}{4}+\alpha\varepsilon}N_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim N_{1}^{-1}(L_{1}L_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{\max}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}N_{1}^{1-\frac{3\alpha}{4}+\alpha\varepsilon}N_{2}^{-1+\frac{3\alpha}{4}-\alpha\varepsilon}N_{2}^{1-\frac{3\alpha}{4}+\alpha\varepsilon+\varepsilon}\\
&\quad\quad\times\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim N_{1}^{-1}(L_{1}L_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{\max}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{N_{1}}{N_{2}}\Big)^{1-\frac{3\alpha}{4}+\alpha\varepsilon}N_{2}^{1-\frac{3\alpha}{4}+(\alpha+1)\varepsilon}\\
&\quad\quad\times\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}},\end{split}

which is summable since
\alpha\geqslant\frac{5}{2}
.

(ii) High
\times
High
\rightarrow
Low
(
N\lesssim N_{1}\sim N_{2}
): The two cases are

•

L_{\max}\ll N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2}
: In case
N\gtrsim 1
, using the nonlinear Loomis–Whitney estimate (
35
), we obtain

\begin{split}\sum_{N\ll N_{1}\sim N_{2}}I&\lesssim\sum_{N\ll N_{1}\sim N_{2}}N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}N_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3\alpha}{4}+\alpha\varepsilon}NN_{1}^{-2s}N^{s}(L_{1}L_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}L^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}\\
&\qquad N^{-1}N_{1}^{s}N_{2}^{s}N^{-s}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&=\sum_{N\ll N_{1}\sim N_{2}}N^{\frac{1}{2}+s+\epsilon}N_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3\alpha}{4}-2s+\alpha\varepsilon}N^{-1}N_{1}^{s}N_{2}^{s}N^{-s}\\
&\qquad\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{N_{1}\sim N_{2}\gtrsim 1}N_{1}^{1-\frac{3\alpha}{4}-s+\alpha\varepsilon+\varepsilon}N^{-1}N_{1}^{s}N_{2}^{s}N^{-s}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

The above can be summed up for
s>1-\frac{3\alpha}{4}
.

•

L_{\max}\gtrsim N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2}
: This case can be handled in the same way as the analogous subcase in High
\times
Low
\rightarrow
High interaction.

(iii) Very low frequencies
(
N,N_{1},N_{2}\lesssim 1
): Using the bilinear Strichartz estimate from Lemma
4.3
, we have

\begin{split}\sum_{N,N_{1},N_{2}\lesssim 1}N\cdot I&\lesssim\sum_{N\sim N_{1}\sim N_{2}\lesssim 1}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}*g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}N\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{N,N_{1},N_{2}\lesssim 1}(L_{1}L_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}(N_{1}\wedge N_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}(N_{1}\vee N_{2})^{\frac{1}{4}}L^{\frac{1}{2}-}\\
&\qquad\times\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}N\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\\
\end{split}

with straight-forward summation.
∎

9.2.
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}
case

We prove the following result:

Theorem 9.3
.

Let
\alpha\geqslant 5
and
s>\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\alpha}{4}
. Then, there is some
b>\frac{1}{2}
such that for
T=T(\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s,0}})
, (
1
) is analytically locally well-posed in
H^{s,0}
with the solution lying in
X^{s,b}_{T}\hookrightarrow C([0,T];H^{s,0})
.

As in the
\mathbb{R}^{2}
case, the claim is implied by the bilinear estimate:

Proposition 9.4
.

Let
\alpha\geqslant 5
and
s>\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\alpha}{4}
. Then there is some
b>\frac{1}{2}
such that the following estimate is true:

(97)

\|\partial_{x}(uv)\|_{X^{s,b-1}}\lesssim\|u\|_{X^{s,b}}\|v\|_{X^{s,b}}.

We prove Proposition
9.4
in a series of lemmata. After dyadic decomposition in the
x
-frequencies, we aim to prove an estimate similar to (
96
) with a suitable, but different summability constant, say
\tilde{C}(N,N_{1},N_{2})
.
We first observe that for
L_{med}\gtrsim N_{\max}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}
, the nonlinear Loomis–Whitney estimate and the bilinear Strichartz estimates are the same as in the
\mathbb{R}^{2}
case. The boundary cases
N_{\max}\lesssim 1
and
N_{\min}\lesssim N_{\max}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}
are treated in Lemma
9.8
.

Hence, it remains to consider the following cases:

•

L_{\max}\lesssim N_{\max}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}
,

•

L_{med}\lesssim N_{\max}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\lesssim L_{\max}\lesssim N_{\max}^{\alpha}N_{\min}
,

•

L_{\max}\gtrsim N_{\max}^{\alpha}N_{\min}
.

The first two cases are sub-cases of the resonant case, while the last case is the non-resonant case. In the following, we assume that

(98)

N_{\max}\gtrsim 1,\text{ and }N_{\min}\gtrsim N_{\max}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}.

We handle the first case in the following:

Lemma 9.5
.

Let
\alpha\geqslant 5
,
s>\frac{1-\alpha}{4}
and
N,N_{i}\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}}
,
L_{i}\in 2^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
, and suppose that (
98
). Let
f_{N_{1},L_{1}},g_{N_{2},L_{2}},h_{N,L}:\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
supp(f_{N_{1},L_{1}})\subseteq D_{N_{1},L_{1}}
,
supp(g_{N_{2},L_{2}})\subseteq D_{N_{2},L_{2}}
, and
supp(h_{N,L})\subseteq D_{N,L}
such that
L_{\max}\lesssim\max(N,N_{1},N_{2})^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}
. Then, the estimate (
97
) holds.

Proof.

To prove the result, we consider the following cases:

(i) Low
\times
High
\rightarrow
High
(
N_{2}\lesssim N_{1}\sim N
): If the size of the low frequency, viz.
N_{2}\lesssim 1
, we consider two cases:
In case
N_{1}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{1}{2}}L_{\max}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim N_{2}\lesssim 1
, we use the Loomis–Whitney estimate (
36
), (note:
L_{\max}\ll N_{1}^{\alpha}N_{2}
) to obtain

\begin{split}\sum_{N_{1}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{1}{2}}L_{\max}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim N_{2}\lesssim 1}I&\lesssim\sum_{N_{1}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{1}{2}}L_{\max}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim N_{2}\lesssim 1}N_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}}N_{2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(L_{\max}L_{\min})^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\quad\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{5}{4}-\frac{\alpha}{4}}N_{1}^{-1}L_{\max}^{\frac{1}{4}}L_{\min}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

Summing up the above in the spatial frequencies gives the required estimate. In case
N_{2}\lesssim N_{1}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{1}{2}}L_{\max}^{\frac{1}{2}}
, we use the bilinear Strichartz estimate (
25
) to obtain

\begin{split}\sum_{N_{2}\lesssim N_{1}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{1}{2}}L_{\max}^{\frac{1}{2}}}I&\lesssim\sum_{N_{2}\lesssim N_{1}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{1}{2}}L_{\max}^{\frac{1}{2}}}N_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{\min}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{5}{4}-\frac{\alpha}{4}}N_{1}^{-1}L_{\min}^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{\max}^{\frac{1}{4}}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

In the case
N_{2}\gtrsim 1
, the nonlinear Loomis–Whitney estimate (
36
) gives

\begin{split}\sum_{1\lesssim N_{2}\lesssim N_{1}}I&\lesssim\sum_{1\lesssim N_{2}\lesssim N_{1}}N_{2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}N_{1}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{1}{2}}(L_{\min}L_{\max})^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{1\lesssim N_{2}\lesssim N_{1}}N_{2}^{-\frac{1}{2}-s}N_{1}^{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\varepsilon^{\prime}}L_{\min}^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{\max}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}N_{2}^{s}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

We observe that in case
s>-\frac{1}{2}
, it is straightforward to sum the above expression in
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
. In case
-\frac{1}{2}-s\geq 0
, we obtain for the above expression that it is bounded by

\sum_{1\lesssim N_{2}\lesssim N_{1}}\Big(\frac{N_{2}}{N_{1}}\Big)^{-\frac{1}{2}-s}N_{1}^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}-s+\varepsilon}N^{-1}L_{\min}^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{\max}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon^{\prime}}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\sup_{N_{2}\gtrsim 1}N_{2}^{s}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}.

It is easy to observe that the above is summable for
s>1-\frac{\alpha}{2}
.

(ii) High
\times
High
\rightarrow
Low
(
N_{1}\sim N_{2}\gtrsim N
): We first consider the case
N\lesssim 1
where the derivative in the nonlinearity is smoothing. Using the nonlinear Loomis–Whitney estimate (
36
), we have

\begin{split}\sum_{N\lesssim 1\lesssim N_{1}\sim N_{2}}I&\lesssim\sum_{N\lesssim 1\lesssim N_{1}\sim N_{2}}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}N_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}}(L_{\min}L_{\max})^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{N\lesssim 1\lesssim N_{1}\sim N_{2}}N^{\frac{1}{2}}N_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2s+\varepsilon}L_{\min}^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{\max}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon^{\prime}}N^{-1}N_{1}^{s}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\quad\quad\times N_{2}^{s}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

We observe that the above is summable for
s>\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\alpha}{2}
.

For
N\gtrsim 1
, the same estimate yields

\begin{split}\sum_{1\lesssim N\lesssim N_{1}\sim N_{2}}I&\lesssim\sum_{1\lesssim N\lesssim N_{1}\sim N_{2}}N^{\frac{1}{2}+s}N_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2s+\varepsilon^{\prime}}L_{\min}^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{\max}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon^{\prime}}N^{-1}N_{1}^{s}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\quad\qquad\qquad\times N_{2}^{s}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}N^{-s}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

The above is summable for
s>\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\alpha}{4}
. We observe that this is the case which determines the regularity threshold for local well-posedness.

∎

Next, we have the following result to deal with the intermediate case
L_{med}\lesssim N_{\max}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\lesssim L_{\max}
:

Lemma 9.6
.

Let
\alpha\geqslant 5
,
s>\frac{1-\alpha}{4}
, and
N,N_{i}\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}}
,
L_{i},L\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}
, and suppose that (
98
). Let
f_{N_{1},L_{1}},g_{N_{2},L_{2}},h_{N,L}:\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
supp(f_{N_{1},L_{1}})\subseteq D_{N_{1},L_{1}}
,
\text{supp}(g_{N_{2},L_{2}})\subseteq D_{N_{2},L_{2}}
,
\text{supp}(h_{N,L})\subseteq D_{N,L}
such that
L_{med}\lesssim N_{\max}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\lesssim L_{\max}
. Then, the estimate (
97
) holds.

Proof.

Note that in this case the Loomis–Whitney estimate remains the same, but the bilinear Strichartz estimate gains
N_{1}^{-\frac{\alpha}{4}}
at the cost of
L_{\max}^{\frac{1}{2}}
.

(i) Low
\times
High
\rightarrow
High
(
N_{2}\lesssim N_{1}\sim N
): In the case
N_{2}\lesssim 1
, we consider two subcases. For
N_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{4}}\lesssim N_{2}
, we use the Loomis-Whitney estimate (
36
) to obtain

\begin{split}\sum_{N_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{4}}\lesssim N_{2}}I&\lesssim\sum_{N_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{4}}\lesssim N_{2}}N^{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\varepsilon}N_{2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(L_{1}L_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}L^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon^{\prime}}N^{-1}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{5}{4}-\frac{3\alpha}{8}+\varepsilon}\sum_{N_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{4}}\lesssim N_{2}}(L_{1}L_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}L^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon^{\prime}}N^{-1}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

In the complementary case viz.
N_{2}\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{4}}
, using the bilinear Strichartz estimate (
25
), we have

\begin{split}\sum_{N_{2}\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{4}}}I&\lesssim\sum_{N_{2}\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{4}}}N_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}N_{1}^{-\frac{\alpha}{4}}(L_{\min}L_{\max})^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{5}{4}-\frac{3\alpha}{8}+\varepsilon}\sum_{N_{2}\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{4}}}(L_{1}L_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}L^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon^{\prime}}N^{-1}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

For
N_{2}\gtrsim 1
, the same estimate as the case
L_{\max}\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}
suffices.

(ii) High
\times
High
\rightarrow
Low
(
N_{1}\sim N_{2}\gtrsim N
):
This case can be dealt with in the same way as the corresponding case in
L_{\max}\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}
since we employ the Loomis–Whitney estimate which is the same for both the cases.

∎

The case
L_{\max}\gtrsim N_{\max}^{\alpha}N_{\min}
is dealt with in the following:

Lemma 9.7
.

Let
\alpha\geqslant 5
,
s>\frac{1-\alpha}{4}
and
N,N_{i}\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}}
,
L_{i},L\in 2^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
, and suppose that (
98
). Let
f_{N_{1},L_{1}},g_{N_{2},L_{2}},h_{N,L}:\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
supp(f_{N_{1},L_{1}})\subseteq D_{N_{1},L_{1}}
,
\text{supp}(g_{N_{2},L_{2}})\subseteq D_{N_{2},L_{2}}
,
\text{supp}(h_{N,L})\subseteq D_{N,L}
such that
L_{\max}\gtrsim N_{\max}^{\alpha}N_{\min}
. Then, the estimate (
97
) holds.

Proof.

(i) Low
\times
High
\rightarrow
High
(
N_{2}\lesssim N_{1}\sim N
): In case
L_{\max}=L_{2}
, using Lemma
4.3
, we have

\begin{split}I&\lesssim\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\ast h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim N_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}(L\wedge L_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle(L\vee L_{1})N_{1}\rangle^{\frac{1}{4}}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim N_{1}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{5}{4}}(L\wedge L_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}(L\vee L_{1})^{\frac{1}{4}}L_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}N^{-1}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

If
N_{2}\lesssim 1
, the above is summable for any
s
. For
N_{2}\gtrsim 1
, the above expression is dominated by

N_{1}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{5}{4}}N_{2}^{-s}(L\wedge L_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}(L\vee L_{1})^{\frac{1}{4}}L_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}N^{-1}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}N_{2}^{s}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}.

If
s\geqslant 0
, the expression is summable in the spatial frequencies. For
s<0
, the above is dominated by

\Big(\frac{N_{1}}{N_{2}}\Big)^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{5}{4}}N_{2}^{-s-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\frac{5}{4}}(L\wedge L_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}(L\vee L_{1})^{\frac{1}{4}}L_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}N^{-1}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}N_{2}^{s}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}

which is summable for
s>\frac{5-\alpha}{4}
.

If
L_{\max}=L
, we use Lemma
4.3
as follows:

\begin{split}I&\lesssim\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\ast g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim N_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}(L_{1}\wedge L_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle(L_{1}\vee L_{2})N_{2}\rangle^{\frac{1}{4}}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim N_{2}^{\frac{1}{4}+\varepsilon}N_{1}^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\varepsilon}(L_{1}\wedge L_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}(L_{1}\vee L_{2})^{\frac{1}{4}}L^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon^{\prime}}N^{-1}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\end{split}

For
N_{2}\lesssim 1
, summability follows since
\alpha\geqslant 5
. For
N_{2}\gtrsim 1
, the above expression is bounded by

\begin{split}N_{2}^{\frac{1}{4}-s+\varepsilon}N_{1}^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\varepsilon}(L_{1}\wedge L_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}(L_{1}\vee L_{2})^{\frac{1}{4}}L^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon^{\prime}}N^{-1}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}N_{2}^{s}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\end{split}

which can be summed up for
s>\frac{5}{4}-\frac{\alpha}{2}
.

(ii) High
\times
High
\rightarrow
Low
(
N\lesssim N_{1}\sim N_{2}
): We first consider the case
L_{\max}=L
. If
N\lesssim 1
, the derivative in the nonlinearity is smoothing and using the bilinear Strichartz estimate from Lemma
4.3
for the high frequencies, we obtain

\begin{split}I&\lesssim\|f_{1,N_{1},L_{1}}*g_{2,N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim(N_{1}^{\alpha}N)^{-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}N^{\frac{1}{2}}N_{1}^{\frac{1}{4}}(L_{1}L_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}L^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\alpha\varepsilon-2s}(L_{1}L_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}L^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}N_{1}^{s}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}N_{2}^{s}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}N^{\varepsilon}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

The above is summable for
s>\frac{1}{8}-\frac{\alpha}{4}
.

For
N\gtrsim 1
, the above estimate is still sufficient and is summable for
s>\frac{1}{8}-\frac{\alpha}{4}
.

If
L_{\max}=L_{1}
, we have using Lemma
4.3
,

\begin{split}I&\lesssim\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\ast h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\\
&\lesssim N^{\frac{1}{2}}(L_{2}\wedge L)^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle(L_{2}\vee L)N\rangle^{\frac{1}{4}}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim NN_{1}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}(L_{2}\wedge L)^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle(L_{2}\vee L)N\rangle^{\frac{1}{4}}L_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}N^{-1}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim NN_{1}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2s}(L_{2}\wedge L)^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle(L_{2}\vee L)N\rangle^{\frac{1}{4}}L_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}N^{-1}N_{1}^{s}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}N_{2}^{s}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

If
N\lesssim 1
, we require
s>-\frac{\alpha}{4}
for summability in spatial frequencies. If
N\gtrsim 1
, the following bound

\begin{split}&\lesssim N^{\frac{5}{4}+s}N_{1}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}-2s}(L_{2}\wedge L)^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle(L_{2}\vee L)N\rangle^{\frac{1}{4}}L_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}N^{-1}N_{1}^{s}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}N_{2}^{s}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\quad\quad\times N^{-s}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\end{split}

implies that we require
s>\frac{-\alpha}{4}
to be able to sum up the above estimate.
∎

The very small frequencies can be handled as follows:

Lemma 9.8
.

Let
\alpha\geqslant 5
,
s>\frac{1-\alpha}{4}
and
N,N_{i}\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}}
such that
N_{\max}\lesssim 1
or
N_{\min}\lesssim N_{\max}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}
. Let
f_{N_{1},L_{1}},g_{N_{2},L_{2}},h_{N,L}:\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
supp(f_{N_{1},L_{1}})\subseteq D_{N_{1},L_{1}}
,
\text{supp}(g_{N_{2},L_{2}})\subseteq D_{N_{2},L_{2}}
,
\text{supp}(h_{N,L})\subseteq D_{N,L}
. Then, the estimate (
97
) holds.

Proof.

In this case, we do not distinguish between the resonant and the non-resonant case. Using Lemma
4.3
, we have

\begin{split}I&\lesssim\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\ast g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim N_{\min}^{\frac{1}{2}}(L_{1}\wedge L_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle(L_{1}\vee L_{2})N_{\max}\rangle^{\frac{1}{4}}\|f_{N_{1},L_{1}}\|_{L^{2}}\|g_{N_{2},L_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|h_{N,L}\|_{L^{2}},\end{split}

which is sufficient.

∎

9.3.
Conclusion of the theorems on semilinear local well-posedness

Proof of Theorem
9.1
.

The proof follows along the same lines as for
[
33
, Theorem 6.1]
, and we shall be brief. We use
X^{s,b}
spaces as the auxiliary spaces to run a fixed point argument for the operator given by:

\Gamma(u)(t):=\eta(t)S_{\alpha}(t)u_{0}+\eta(t)\int_{0}^{t}S_{\alpha}(t-s)(u\partial_{x}u)(s)ds.

Here,
\eta
is a smooth compactly supported time cut-off. Using the linear estimate, the energy estimate for
X^{s,b}
spaces
[
35
, Section 2.6]
and Proposition
9.2
, we obtain

\|\Gamma(u)\|_{X^{s,b}_{1}}\leqslant C(\|u(0)\|_{H^{s,0}}+\|u\|_{X^{s,b}_{1}}^{2}).

Similarly,

\|\Gamma(u_{1})-\Gamma(u_{2})\|_{X^{s,b}_{1}}\leqslant 2\tilde{C}(\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s,0}})\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{X^{s,b}_{1}}.

For small initial data (attributed in the constants
C,\tilde{C}
), we can prove local well-posedness for (
51
) on
X^{s,b}_{1}
. Using scaling and subcriticality of the regularity, any large data can be scaled to be small and one obtains well-posedness on a time interval
[0,T]
, with
T
depending on the size of the initial data.
∎

In preparation of the proof, we recall the following lemma to trade regularity in modulation to powers of time:

Lemma 9.9
.

Let
\eta\in\mathbb{R}
,
-\frac{1}{2}<b^{\prime}\leq b<\frac{1}{2}
, then for any
0<T<1
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, it holds

\|\eta(t/T)u\|_{X^{s,b^{\prime}}}\lesssim_{\eta,b,b^{\prime}}T^{b-b^{\prime}}\|u\|_{X^{s,b}}.

Proof of Theorem
9.3
.

For small initial data and a fixed time interval
[0,1]
, the proof of local well-posedness is the same as in the
\mathbb{R}^{2}
case. In the large data case, we use the leeway in the modulation regularity in the proof of Proposition
9.4
to apply Lemma
9.9
. This yields a modified bilinear estimate

\|\partial_{x}(uv)\|_{X^{s,b-1}_{T}}\lesssim T^{\delta}\|u\|_{X^{s,b}}\|v\|_{X^{s,b}}.

Thus, we see that the time of the existence of the solution will depend on the size of the initial data and the well-posedness result follows similarly as in the
\mathbb{R}^{2}
case.
∎
